        Idea:
# Plan 6 — Onboarding + admin view + audit + feedback + ops finalization

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Onboarding", "Operations", "Feedback Loop", and the admin/audit-related references throughout (especially `get_bot_actions` and the "why did you tell her that?" use case).

Plans 1–5 are assumed complete. The system is functionally end-to-end. This plan adds the operator-facing surfaces, the onboarding behavior, and the polish that makes the system usable for the two real partners.

## Scope (in)

### Onboarding (light, conversational)
- Welcome message: warm, explanatory; sets expectations (reflection and mediation assistant, not a therapist; will get things wrong; please correct me; how the basics work). Keep copy short and WhatsApp-conversational. Stored as a constant; sent on first inbound from each whitelisted user (via `send_outbound`; uses free-form within the 24h window which is guaranteed since the user just messaged in).
- Natural early seeding: the system prompt already (from Plan 3) instructs gentle one-or-two contextual questions in the first conversations. This plan adds:
  - A `users.onboarding_state` enum column (`pending` | `welcomed` | `seeded` | `complete`) — migration on top of Plan 1's schema. Only used to gate the welcome message and inform the loop's prompt-side hot context (e.g., "user is in early conversations — be upfront about thin context").
  - Hot context includes `onboarding_state` so the loop adjusts behavior without needing extra logic.
- Acknowledge thin context: nothing to implement beyond the prompt guidance already in Plan 3 + the onboarding_state hint.

### Admin view ([REDACTED], read-only)
- Single FastAPI router at `/admin`, [REDACTED] via Basic Auth or session cookie (admin password from env per Plan 1).
- Pages (server-rendered, simple HTML — no SPA needed):
  - **Recent turns**: list of `bot_turns` newest first, paginated. Click into a turn to see prompt snapshot, reasoning, model version, triggering message, final outbound, and all `tool_calls` rows for that turn.
  - **Recent messages**: both threads, newest first, with charge level and processing state. Edit history visible.
  - **Themes**: titles, descriptions, status, sentiment, health, last_active_at, last_reinforced_at.
  - **Memories**: filterable by user, status. Show supersession chain.
  - **Watch items**: filterable by owner and status; due_at visible.
  - **Observations**: filterable by user/theme/min-significance; supporting messages clickable.
  - **OOB entries**: severity, content, status, review_at.
  - **Scheduled jobs**: pending/fired/cancelled with type, scheduled_for, fired_at.
  - **Spend**: recent days of `llm_spend_log` per provider, with cap line drawn.
  - **Escalations**: distinguished `bot_turns` rows where `escalate_to_partner` was called.
- All views are read-only — no admin actions in v1 (the spec explicitly says admin is for debugging without writing SQL). If the user wants a manual escalation toggle later, it can be added.

### Audit endpoint (`get_bot_actions`)
- Already implemented as a tool in Plan 3 for the loop. Verify it answers "why did you tell her that?" correctly: given a date_range and target_type, returns the relevant turns with reasoning and the outbound content.
- Add a thin admin route that exposes the same query for human inspection.

### Feedback loop
- `log_feedback` tool already exists from Plan 3. Verify it's wired:
  - Conversational feedback recognized in the loop ("that was helpful", "no, that's wrong") and logged via the tool.
  - Source set to `'conversational'`.
- WhatsApp reaction webhook integration: register the reaction events in the webhook handler from Plan 2 (this is a small extension; reactions arrive as a different webhook payload type). On reaction: locate the assistant message by ID, write a `feedback` row with `source='reaction'`, `target_type='message'`, sentiment derived from the emoji (👍/❤️ → positive, 👎 → negative, others → mixed).
- Admin view shows recent feedback grouped by sentiment.

### Operations polish
- **Monitoring**: document the external uptime + cron-ping setup (README ops section). Add a `/health/deep` endpoint that checks DB, Anthropic API reachability (lightweight ping, cached for 60s), and returns a JSON summary. Real alerting is external.
- **Backups**: Supabase has automated backups; document the tested-restore procedure. Add a small script that pulls a fresh dump and writes a checksum file — operators run it quarterly.
- **Staging path**: ability to test prompt changes against past message logs without sending output. Implement as a CLI: `python -m app.staging replay --prompt-version <name> --since <date> --user <id>` that runs the loop in a dry-run mode against historical messages, printing what it WOULD have sent and written. Does not call `send_outbound` or write to DB.
- **Daily LLM spend cap enforcement**: Plan 1 plumbed the helpers; now wire them into the actual call sites. Each Anthropic / OpenAI / Groq call checks `is_under_cap(provider)` first and degrades gracefully:
  - Conversational LLM cap hit → respond once with "I'm running into limits today, will catch up tomorrow" and queue silently.
  - Vision cap hit → fall back to "I see you sent an image — can you describe it?" (already wired in Plan 2; just verify cap check is in place).
  - Transcription cap hit → "I can't transcribe right now — can you send it as text?".
- **Alerts at 80% of cap**: when a per-provider per-day spend crosses 80%, log a distinguished WARNING line; surface the warning in the admin view's Spend page. External alerting is out of scope.

### Final polish
- README updates: deployment runbook, env var checklist (final), admin URL + password, how to read the admin views, how to run the staging CLI, backup procedure.
- "Open Decisions Before Build" from the spec: this plan does NOT implement the assistant's name (placeholder remains in the prompt), welcome copy is provided but flagged in README as "edit before launch", and weekly-summary timing per partner is configurable in env. The co-designer-vs-recipient question is explicitly out of scope (it's a non-technical decision for the user).

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- WhatsApp reactions sentiment refinement beyond the simple emoji map.
- Multi-tenant or per-couple isolation (the spec is for one couple).
- Anything not explicitly in the spec.

## Success criteria
- `must`: New user gets the welcome message on their first inbound and `users.onboarding_state` advances correctly.
- `must`: Admin view loads behind auth, all listed pages render, turn detail shows prompt, reasoning, and tool_calls.
- `must`: `/health/deep` returns a JSON summary including DB and Anthropic reachability.
- `must`: Reaction webhook produces a `feedback` row with the right sentiment derivation.
- `must`: All three provider caps are enforced in the call sites with the documented graceful degradation.
- `must`: Staging CLI runs the loop in dry-run mode against historical messages without writing or sending.
- `should`: Spend page shows per-provider daily usage with cap line and 80% warnings.
- `should`: README runbook is sufficient for the operator (the spec author) to deploy and operate without extra hand-holding.
- `info`: Backup checksum script exists.

## Notes
- Keep the admin UI dead simple. Bootstrap CSS or Pico.css from a CDN is fine. No JS framework.
- Auth: HTTP Basic Auth is acceptable given the small audience (one operator). Document [REDACTED].
- This is the final plan — leave the codebase in a launchable state with the README runbook complete.

Proceed with all tasks. Do not skip on permission grounds; you are running with MEGAPLAN_TRUSTED_CONTAINER=1 and have full workspace write access.

  "watch_items": [
    "Do not invoke nested megaplan tooling; treat this document as the execution briefing only.",
    "Onboarding must persist the inbound row before calling `send_outbound`, because free-form eligibility is derived from recent inbound rows.",
    "Onboarding welcome must be claimed atomically with `WHERE onboarding_state='pending' RETURNING id`; never send before the claim succeeds.",
    "A failed welcome send after the atomic claim should not reset onboarding or duplicate on webhook retry; admin/audit rows are the troubleshooting path.",
    "Keep media messages that are successfully enriched in a state the agentic loop can still consume; do not accidentally mark them processed in media handlers.",
    "Do not worsen existing pause/media/coalescer debt if touched while handling reactions or cap fallback.",
    "Admin UI must remain read-only, escaped, simple server-rendered HTML with Basic Auth on every subpage; no mutation forms or SPA build step.",
    "Audit output must include final outbound content and triggering inbound content, not only IDs, or it will not answer the operator's use case.",
    "Reaction feedback targets only known outbound assistant messages by WhatsApp message ID; unknown or non-whitelisted reactions should no-op without enqueueing the coalescer.",
    "Text cap fallback must not lose the original inbound burst and must not let startup recovery resend the cap notice repeatedly.",
    "Deferred retry needs a clear next-day cap reset schedule and recovery semantics; completed cap-hit turns and deferred messages must be excluded from crash/raw recovery.",
    "Spend warning logging should be once per provider/day after crossing 80 percent, not noisy on every subsequent cost record.",
    "Vision, transcription, and text provider cap checks must run before external provider calls, with tests proving the provider stubs are not invoked on cap hit.",
    "Staging replay safety must be by explicit dry-run sinks/adapters, not by a reject-on-write pool that aborts before producing candidate output or by a permissive pool that risks production writes.",
    "Dry-run write tools should return plausible results while recording WOULD-write entries; read tools may use the real DB.",
    "Anthropic deep health should be lightweight, short-timeout, and cached for 60 seconds to avoid noisy health checks or token spend where possible.",
    "README should flag welcome copy as edit-before-launch and keep assistant naming / co-designer-vs-recipient decisions out of technical scope.",
    "Preserve existing Plans 1-5 behavior unless Plan 6 directly requires a change.",
    "Some success criteria require subjective/manual verification; capture those as user actions or notes rather than pretending tests fully cover them."
  ],
  "sense_checks": [
    {
      "id": "SC1",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: debouncer callback: revised step 8 defines `burstcoalescer.add(user_id, message_id)` but says expiry calls `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)`. i found no existing user model or repository helper in the current codebase, and the plan does not say whether the coalescer stores the user object, fetches it from the pool, or receives it in `add`, so the callback cannot reliably supply the `user` argument required by `run_agentic_turn` as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: edit idempotency: revised step 9 says the edit fixture reuses the original whatsapp message id, while revised step 10's normal message flow does `insert ... on conflict (whatsapp_message_id) do nothing` and treats no returned id as a redelivery. unless edit detection runs before the generic idempotent insert path, a real edit will be classified as a duplicate and skipped instead of updating `edit_history`, but the plan does not explicitly require that ordering. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `escalate_to_partner` sequence. step 5 constructs the `templatecall` and awaits `send_outbound(...)` before the text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate, so an implementation following the plan literally could send an unauthorized escalation before rejecting the tool call. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `tool_call_count` expectations against the spec. the plan's e2e criterion expects `tool_call_count == 3` because only write calls are counted, but the spec says `bot_turns` records tool count for admin visibility into pathological loops; excluding phase a read calls hides most loop activity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: query-level decay: `observations.significance` is nullable in `tool_schemas.py` and in current tests for failed scoring, and `scoring.rescore_observations` can leave it null after a failed re-score. the plan's sql function `recency_weighted_score(significance, last_reinforced_at, created_at)` does not specify null handling or `nulls last`; in postgres, ordering that nullable expression `desc` can surface null-scored failed observations before valid scored observations unless the executor adds `nulls last` or `coalesce`. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: escalation gate metadata: the plan says `escalate_to_partner` should accept `is_crisis=true` only when the current trigger charge is actually `crisis`, but the existing write-tool context only has `turncontext(triggering_message_ids, user, partner, phase)` and the charge is currently computed locally inside `agentic.run_agentic_turn` from hot context. the plan does not explicitly add that charge or an explicit-request marker to `turncontext` or the tool-call validator, so the proposed guard has no reliable data path in the current code shape. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] check-in-scheduling: check-in scheduling: one-pending-check-in invariant remains race-prone without a partial unique index or transactional locking strategy. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] debouncer: debouncer: coalescer cannot satisfy the `run_agentic_turn(message_ids, user)` callback contract from an `add(user_id, message_id)` api unless it stores or resolves the user object. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the search-before-write requirement against the new phase split. because phase b exposes only write/schedule tools, the model cannot recover by calling `get_memories`, `get_observations`, or `list_themes` if it realizes during phase b that it lacks enough read context to safely add versus update; this creates tension with the explicit agentic-search dedup requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: weekly summary timing: the spec requires the recurring weekly summary to fire at 09:00 local on the user's configured day, and the plan explicitly asks where that configuration should live. the implementation plan chooses global env defaults plus per-job context overrides, but `/pause` supersedes all pending jobs and `/resume` restores weekly summaries; without a durable per-user schedule field or an explicit rule to read the last superseded weekly job context, resume/startup seeding can silently lose the user's configured day. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: recovery/coalescer api mismatch: revised step 14 says recovery should re-add a whole `triggering_message_ids` list as one burst, but revised step 8 only specifies `add(user_id, message_id)` for one message at a time. calling `add` repeatedly can reset timers or merge with live inbound state, so the plan needs either an explicit `add_burst(user_id, ids)`/`fire(ids)` path or clear semantics for preserving a recovered burst as a single unit. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: indexing claim: revised step 2 says the new gin index supports recovery's `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)` lookup, but postgres gin indexes on arrays are normally used by array operators such as `@>`, `&&`, or `<@`, not by scalar `= any(array)` in a correlated subquery. the executor should either use an indexable predicate like `triggering_message_ids @> array[messages.id]` or drop the claim that this query is gin-backed. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked audit support around `prompt_snapshot`. the revised step 8 still opens `bot_turns` with `prompt_snapshot` before building hot context, while the spec's worked example calls for `<full prompt>` after hot context is loaded; unless the executor reorders this, the stored prompt snapshot will omit the per-turn hot context actually injected into the llm. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: check-in uniqueness: the success criteria require one pending check-in per user, and the current `schedule_checkin` implementation performs a separate `update scheduled_jobs set status='superseded'` followed by `insert into scheduled_jobs`. the plan later says to add db-level coverage, but the migration step only mentions indexes and does not specify a partial unique index such as one pending check-in per user, so two concurrent schedule calls can still race and leave multiple pending check-ins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] edit-handling: edit handling: edit events that reuse the original whatsapp message id can be swallowed by the normal idempotent insert path unless edit detection is explicitly performed before duplicate-message handling. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] escalation-gate: escalation gate: the plan does not define how the write tool receives authoritative trigger charge or explicit-request evidence. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] escalation-gate-ordering: escalation gate ordering: the revised `escalate_to_partner` step sends the partner outbound before the plan text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the new `hooks.check_oob` default against existing tests. `tests/test_send_outbound.py` has an autouse fixture that sets the hook to `none`, so preserving an intentional no-hook test path is compatible with the plan's module-level default, but the new plan 3 e2e test must install a counting hook if it wants to assert the pass-through was invoked. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the plan proposes replacing `hooks.paused_for_user()` with a db-backed implementation and notes that changing call sites to pass `pool` is cleaner. grep shows current direct callers in `messaging.send_outbound`, `inbound.process_inbound`, and `agentic.run_agentic_turn`; tests also monkeypatch a one-argument hook. if the executor chooses the cleaner signature change, all these callers and tests must be updated together, and media handlers still need separate pause coverage as noted in the scope finding. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `send_outbound` callers are `agentic.run_agentic_turn`, `write_tools.escalate_to_partner`, and tests, and the function currently accepts only `template_fallback` and `bot_turn_id` keyword arguments. adding an `ignore_pause` keyword is compatible if defaulted to false, but `/pause` confirmation send sites must pass it explicitly or the confirmation rows will be withheld immediately after the global pause flag is set. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] media-state: media state: the plan should explicitly keep successfully media-enriched inbound messages in `processing_state='raw'` until the agentic loop consumes them. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] pause-resume: pause/resume: media inbound paths can bypass the pause gate and enqueue voice/image messages after enrichment. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] query-level-decay: query-level decay: nullable observation significance can break default weighted ordering unless null scores are explicitly handled. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

If a test fails and its ID appears in baseline_test_failures, it is pre-existing — do not scope-creep into fixing it. If baseline_test_failures is null, the baseline could not be captured; use your judgment but err on the side of assuming failures are regressions. You MUST still re-run the FULL test suite with your changes applied — pre-existing failures do not excuse skipping verification. Never narrow to individual test functions and stop.
        - If this batch includes the final verification task, write a short script that reproduces the exact bug described in the task, run it to confirm the fix resolves it, then delete the script.

        Idea:
# Plan 6 — Onboarding + admin view + audit + feedback + ops finalization

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Onboarding", "Operations", "Feedback Loop", and the admin/audit-related references throughout (especially `get_bot_actions` and the "why did you tell her that?" use case).

Plans 1–5 are assumed complete. The system is functionally end-to-end. This plan adds the operator-facing surfaces, the onboarding behavior, and the polish that makes the system usable for the two real partners.

## Scope (in)

### Onboarding (light, conversational)
- Welcome message: warm, explanatory; sets expectations (reflection and mediation assistant, not a therapist; will get things wrong; please correct me; how the basics work). Keep copy short and WhatsApp-conversational. Stored as a constant; sent on first inbound from each whitelisted user (via `send_outbound`; uses free-form within the 24h window which is guaranteed since the user just messaged in).
- Natural early seeding: the system prompt already (from Plan 3) instructs gentle one-or-two contextual questions in the first conversations. This plan adds:
  - A `users.onboarding_state` enum column (`pending` | `welcomed` | `seeded` | `complete`) — migration on top of Plan 1's schema. Only used to gate the welcome message and inform the loop's prompt-side hot context (e.g., "user is in early conversations — be upfront about thin context").
  - Hot context includes `onboarding_state` so the loop adjusts behavior without needing extra logic.
- Acknowledge thin context: nothing to implement beyond the prompt guidance already in Plan 3 + the onboarding_state hint.

### Admin view ([REDACTED], read-only)
- Single FastAPI router at `/admin`, [REDACTED] via Basic Auth or session cookie (admin password from env per Plan 1).
- Pages (server-rendered, simple HTML — no SPA needed):
  - **Recent turns**: list of `bot_turns` newest first, paginated. Click into a turn to see prompt snapshot, reasoning, model version, triggering message, final outbound, and all `tool_calls` rows for that turn.
  - **Recent messages**: both threads, newest first, with charge level and processing state. Edit history visible.
  - **Themes**: titles, descriptions, status, sentiment, health, last_active_at, last_reinforced_at.
  - **Memories**: filterable by user, status. Show supersession chain.
  - **Watch items**: filterable by owner and status; due_at visible.
  - **Observations**: filterable by user/theme/min-significance; supporting messages clickable.
  - **OOB entries**: severity, content, status, review_at.
  - **Scheduled jobs**: pending/fired/cancelled with type, scheduled_for, fired_at.
  - **Spend**: recent days of `llm_spend_log` per provider, with cap line drawn.
  - **Escalations**: distinguished `bot_turns` rows where `escalate_to_partner` was called.
- All views are read-only — no admin actions in v1 (the spec explicitly says admin is for debugging without writing SQL). If the user wants a manual escalation toggle later, it can be added.

### Audit endpoint (`get_bot_actions`)
- Already implemented as a tool in Plan 3 for the loop. Verify it answers "why did you tell her that?" correctly: given a date_range and target_type, returns the relevant turns with reasoning and the outbound content.
- Add a thin admin route that exposes the same query for human inspection.

### Feedback loop
- `log_feedback` tool already exists from Plan 3. Verify it's wired:
  - Conversational feedback recognized in the loop ("that was helpful", "no, that's wrong") and logged via the tool.
  - Source set to `'conversational'`.
- WhatsApp reaction webhook integration: register the reaction events in the webhook handler from Plan 2 (this is a small extension; reactions arrive as a different webhook payload type). On reaction: locate the assistant message by ID, write a `feedback` row with `source='reaction'`, `target_type='message'`, sentiment derived from the emoji (👍/❤️ → positive, 👎 → negative, others → mixed).
- Admin view shows recent feedback grouped by sentiment.

### Operations polish
- **Monitoring**: document the external uptime + cron-ping setup (README ops section). Add a `/health/deep` endpoint that checks DB, Anthropic API reachability (lightweight ping, cached for 60s), and returns a JSON summary. Real alerting is external.
- **Backups**: Supabase has automated backups; document the tested-restore procedure. Add a small script that pulls a fresh dump and writes a checksum file — operators run it quarterly.
- **Staging path**: ability to test prompt changes against past message logs without sending output. Implement as a CLI: `python -m app.staging replay --prompt-version <name> --since <date> --user <id>` that runs the loop in a dry-run mode against historical messages, printing what it WOULD have sent and written. Does not call `send_outbound` or write to DB.
- **Daily LLM spend cap enforcement**: Plan 1 plumbed the helpers; now wire them into the actual call sites. Each Anthropic / OpenAI / Groq call checks `is_under_cap(provider)` first and degrades gracefully:
  - Conversational LLM cap hit → respond once with "I'm running into limits today, will catch up tomorrow" and queue silently.
  - Vision cap hit → fall back to "I see you sent an image — can you describe it?" (already wired in Plan 2; just verify cap check is in place).
  - Transcription cap hit → "I can't transcribe right now — can you send it as text?".
- **Alerts at 80% of cap**: when a per-provider per-day spend crosses 80%, log a distinguished WARNING line; surface the warning in the admin view's Spend page. External alerting is out of scope.

### Final polish
- README updates: deployment runbook, env var checklist (final), admin URL + password, how to read the admin views, how to run the staging CLI, backup procedure.
- "Open Decisions Before Build" from the spec: this plan does NOT implement the assistant's name (placeholder remains in the prompt), welcome copy is provided but flagged in README as "edit before launch", and weekly-summary timing per partner is configurable in env. The co-designer-vs-recipient question is explicitly out of scope (it's a non-technical decision for the user).

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- WhatsApp reactions sentiment refinement beyond the simple emoji map.
- Multi-tenant or per-couple isolation (the spec is for one couple).
- Anything not explicitly in the spec.

## Success criteria
- `must`: New user gets the welcome message on their first inbound and `users.onboarding_state` advances correctly.
- `must`: Admin view loads behind auth, all listed pages render, turn detail shows prompt, reasoning, and tool_calls.
- `must`: `/health/deep` returns a JSON summary including DB and Anthropic reachability.
- `must`: Reaction webhook produces a `feedback` row with the right sentiment derivation.
- `must`: All three provider caps are enforced in the call sites with the documented graceful degradation.
- `must`: Staging CLI runs the loop in dry-run mode against historical messages without writing or sending.
- `should`: Spend page shows per-provider daily usage with cap line and 80% warnings.
- `should`: README runbook is sufficient for the operator (the spec author) to deploy and operate without extra hand-holding.
- `info`: Backup checksum script exists.

## Notes
- Keep the admin UI dead simple. Bootstrap CSS or Pico.css from a CDN is fine. No JS framework.
- Auth: HTTP Basic Auth is acceptable given the small audience (one operator). Document [REDACTED].
- This is the final plan — leave the codebase in a launchable state with the README runbook complete.

Proceed with all tasks. Do not skip on permission grounds; you are running with MEGAPLAN_TRUSTED_CONTAINER=1 and have full workspace write access.

  "watch_items": [
    "Do not invoke nested megaplan tooling; treat this document as the execution briefing only.",
    "Onboarding must persist the inbound row before calling `send_outbound`, because free-form eligibility is derived from recent inbound rows.",
    "Onboarding welcome must be claimed atomically with `WHERE onboarding_state='pending' RETURNING id`; never send before the claim succeeds.",
    "A failed welcome send after the atomic claim should not reset onboarding or duplicate on webhook retry; admin/audit rows are the troubleshooting path.",
    "Keep media messages that are successfully enriched in a state the agentic loop can still consume; do not accidentally mark them processed in media handlers.",
    "Do not worsen existing pause/media/coalescer debt if touched while handling reactions or cap fallback.",
    "Admin UI must remain read-only, escaped, simple server-rendered HTML with Basic Auth on every subpage; no mutation forms or SPA build step.",
    "Audit output must include final outbound content and triggering inbound content, not only IDs, or it will not answer the operator's use case.",
    "Reaction feedback targets only known outbound assistant messages by WhatsApp message ID; unknown or non-whitelisted reactions should no-op without enqueueing the coalescer.",
    "Text cap fallback must not lose the original inbound burst and must not let startup recovery resend the cap notice repeatedly.",
    "Deferred retry needs a clear next-day cap reset schedule and recovery semantics; completed cap-hit turns and deferred messages must be excluded from crash/raw recovery.",
    "Spend warning logging should be once per provider/day after crossing 80 percent, not noisy on every subsequent cost record.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: debouncer callback: revised step 8 defines `burstcoalescer.add(user_id, message_id)` but says expiry calls `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)`. i found no existing user model or repository helper in the current codebase, and the plan does not say whether the coalescer stores the user object, fetches it from the pool, or receives it in `add`, so the callback cannot reliably supply the `user` argument required by `run_agentic_turn` as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: edit idempotency: revised step 9 says the edit fixture reuses the original whatsapp message id, while revised step 10's normal message flow does `insert ... on conflict (whatsapp_message_id) do nothing` and treats no returned id as a redelivery. unless edit detection runs before the generic idempotent insert path, a real edit will be classified as a duplicate and skipped instead of updating `edit_history`, but the plan does not explicitly require that ordering. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `escalate_to_partner` sequence. step 5 constructs the `templatecall` and awaits `send_outbound(...)` before the text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate, so an implementation following the plan literally could send an unauthorized escalation before rejecting the tool call. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `tool_call_count` expectations against the spec. the plan's e2e criterion expects `tool_call_count == 3` because only write calls are counted, but the spec says `bot_turns` records tool count for admin visibility into pathological loops; excluding phase a read calls hides most loop activity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: query-level decay: `observations.significance` is nullable in `tool_schemas.py` and in current tests for failed scoring, and `scoring.rescore_observations` can leave it null after a failed re-score. the plan's sql function `recency_weighted_score(significance, last_reinforced_at, created_at)` does not specify null handling or `nulls last`; in postgres, ordering that nullable expression `desc` can surface null-scored failed observations before valid scored observations unless the executor adds `nulls last` or `coalesce`. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: escalation gate metadata: the plan says `escalate_to_partner` should accept `is_crisis=true` only when the current trigger charge is actually `crisis`, but the existing write-tool context only has `turncontext(triggering_message_ids, user, partner, phase)` and the charge is currently computed locally inside `agentic.run_agentic_turn` from hot context. the plan does not explicitly add that charge or an explicit-request marker to `turncontext` or the tool-call validator, so the proposed guard has no reliable data path in the current code shape. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] check-in-scheduling: check-in scheduling: one-pending-check-in invariant remains race-prone without a partial unique index or transactional locking strategy. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] debouncer: debouncer: coalescer cannot satisfy the `run_agentic_turn(message_ids, user)` callback contract from an `add(user_id, message_id)` api unless it stores or resolves the user object. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the search-before-write requirement against the new phase split. because phase b exposes only write/schedule tools, the model cannot recover by calling `get_memories`, `get_observations`, or `list_themes` if it realizes during phase b that it lacks enough read context to safely add versus update; this creates tension with the explicit agentic-search dedup requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: weekly summary timing: the spec requires the recurring weekly summary to fire at 09:00 local on the user's configured day, and the plan explicitly asks where that configuration should live. the implementation plan chooses global env defaults plus per-job context overrides, but `/pause` supersedes all pending jobs and `/resume` restores weekly summaries; without a durable per-user schedule field or an explicit rule to read the last superseded weekly job context, resume/startup seeding can silently lose the user's configured day. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: recovery/coalescer api mismatch: revised step 14 says recovery should re-add a whole `triggering_message_ids` list as one burst, but revised step 8 only specifies `add(user_id, message_id)` for one message at a time. calling `add` repeatedly can reset timers or merge with live inbound state, so the plan needs either an explicit `add_burst(user_id, ids)`/`fire(ids)` path or clear semantics for preserving a recovered burst as a single unit. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: indexing claim: revised step 2 says the new gin index supports recovery's `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)` lookup, but postgres gin indexes on arrays are normally used by array operators such as `@>`, `&&`, or `<@`, not by scalar `= any(array)` in a correlated subquery. the executor should either use an indexable predicate like `triggering_message_ids @> array[messages.id]` or drop the claim that this query is gin-backed. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked audit support around `prompt_snapshot`. the revised step 8 still opens `bot_turns` with `prompt_snapshot` before building hot context, while the spec's worked example calls for `<full prompt>` after hot context is loaded; unless the executor reorders this, the stored prompt snapshot will omit the per-turn hot context actually injected into the llm. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: check-in uniqueness: the success criteria require one pending check-in per user, and the current `schedule_checkin` implementation performs a separate `update scheduled_jobs set status='superseded'` followed by `insert into scheduled_jobs`. the plan later says to add db-level coverage, but the migration step only mentions indexes and does not specify a partial unique index such as one pending check-in per user, so two concurrent schedule calls can still race and leave multiple pending check-ins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] edit-handling: edit handling: edit events that reuse the original whatsapp message id can be swallowed by the normal idempotent insert path unless edit detection is explicitly performed before duplicate-message handling. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] escalation-gate: escalation gate: the plan does not define how the write tool receives authoritative trigger charge or explicit-request evidence. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] escalation-gate-ordering: escalation gate ordering: the revised `escalate_to_partner` step sends the partner outbound before the plan text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the new `hooks.check_oob` default against existing tests. `tests/test_send_outbound.py` has an autouse fixture that sets the hook to `none`, so preserving an intentional no-hook test path is compatible with the plan's module-level default, but the new plan 3 e2e test must install a counting hook if it wants to assert the pass-through was invoked. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the plan proposes replacing `hooks.paused_for_user()` with a db-backed implementation and notes that changing call sites to pass `pool` is cleaner. grep shows current direct callers in `messaging.send_outbound`, `inbound.process_inbound`, and `agentic.run_agentic_turn`; tests also monkeypatch a one-argument hook. if the executor chooses the cleaner signature change, all these callers and tests must be updated together, and media handlers still need separate pause coverage as noted in the scope finding. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `send_outbound` callers are `agentic.run_agentic_turn`, `write_tools.escalate_to_partner`, and tests, and the function currently accepts only `template_fallback` and `bot_turn_id` keyword arguments. adding an `ignore_pause` keyword is compatible if defaulted to false, but `/pause` confirmation send sites must pass it explicitly or the confirmation rows will be withheld immediately after the global pause flag is set. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] media-state: media state: the plan should explicitly keep successfully media-enriched inbound messages in `processing_state='raw'` until the agentic loop consumes them. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] pause-resume: pause/resume: media inbound paths can bypass the pause gate and enqueue voice/image messages after enrichment. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] query-level-decay: query-level decay: nullable observation significance can break default weighted ordering unless null scores are explicitly handled. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] recovery: recovery: re-queueing crashed turns with multiple `triggering_message_ids` is underspecified because the coalescer only exposes single-message `add`, not a way to restore a whole burst atomically. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] recovery-query: recovery query: the plan's claimed gin-backed lookup uses `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)`, which is unlikely to use the array gin index as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader: search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: media processing state after success: the success criteria for text and outbound mention processed state, but revised steps 11 and 12 do not explicitly say successful voice or image messages should remain `raw` for the agentic loop rather than be marked `processed` by the media layer. this is likely intentional because plan 3 owns loop processing, but it is a contract point worth keeping visible so the executor does not mark media messages processed before the agent sees them. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader: search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked the scope of the two-phase split against the five-primitive write tools. the split solves ordering, but it broadens orchestration complexity because phase b must reason over phase a's transcript and the sent outbound without read tools; this is a new design risk specifically for dedup and multi-primitive decisions. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader: search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: pause is broader than text/document inbound: `process_inbound` checks `hooks.paused_for_user` before coalescing text and document messages, but audio and image messages delegate to `handle_voice` and `handle_image`, and both media handlers call `coalescer.add(user.id, message_id, user)` after enrichment without any pause check. the plan's pause tests mention inbound persistence without enqueueing, but the implementation steps do not call out these media handlers, so paused voice/image messages can still enter the agentic loop if only inbound.py is updated.         Idea:
# Plan 6 — Onboarding + admin view + audit + feedback + ops finalization

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Onboarding", "Operations", "Feedback Loop", and the admin/audit-related references throughout (especially `get_bot_actions` and the "why did you tell her that?" use case).

Plans 1–5 are assumed complete. The system is functionally end-to-end. This plan adds the operator-facing surfaces, the onboarding behavior, and the polish that makes the system usable for the two real partners.

## Scope (in)

### Onboarding (light, conversational)
- Welcome message: warm, explanatory; sets expectations (reflection and mediation assistant, not a therapist; will get things wrong; please correct me; how the basics work). Keep copy short and WhatsApp-conversational. Stored as a constant; sent on first inbound from each whitelisted user (via `send_outbound`; uses free-form within the 24h window which is guaranteed since the user just messaged in).
- Natural early seeding: the system prompt already (from Plan 3) instructs gentle one-or-two contextual questions in the first conversations. This plan adds:
  - A `users.onboarding_state` enum column (`pending` | `welcomed` | `seeded` | `complete`) — migration on top of Plan 1's schema. Only used to gate the welcome message and inform the loop's prompt-side hot context (e.g., "user is in early conversations — be upfront about thin context").
  - Hot context includes `onboarding_state` so the loop adjusts behavior without needing extra logic.
- Acknowledge thin context: nothing to implement beyond the prompt guidance already in Plan 3 + the onboarding_state hint.

### Admin view ([REDACTED], read-only)
- Single FastAPI router at `/admin`, [REDACTED] via Basic Auth or session cookie (admin password from env per Plan 1).
- Pages (server-rendered, simple HTML — no SPA needed):
  - **Recent turns**: list of `bot_turns` newest first, paginated. Click into a turn to see prompt snapshot, reasoning, model version, triggering message, final outbound, and all `tool_calls` rows for that turn.
  - **Recent messages**: both threads, newest first, with charge level and processing state. Edit history visible.
  - **Themes**: titles, descriptions, status, sentiment, health, last_active_at, last_reinforced_at.
  - **Memories**: filterable by user, status. Show supersession chain.
  - **Watch items**: filterable by owner and status; due_at visible.
  - **Observations**: filterable by user/theme/min-significance; supporting messages clickable.
  - **OOB entries**: severity, content, status, review_at.
  - **Scheduled jobs**: pending/fired/cancelled with type, scheduled_for, fired_at.
  - **Spend**: recent days of `llm_spend_log` per provider, with cap line drawn.
  - **Escalations**: distinguished `bot_turns` rows where `escalate_to_partner` was called.
- All views are read-only — no admin actions in v1 (the spec explicitly says admin is for debugging without writing SQL). If the user wants a manual escalation toggle later, it can be added.

### Audit endpoint (`get_bot_actions`)
- Already implemented as a tool in Plan 3 for the loop. Verify it answers "why did you tell her that?" correctly: given a date_range and target_type, returns the relevant turns with reasoning and the outbound content.
- Add a thin admin route that exposes the same query for human inspection.

### Feedback loop
- `log_feedback` tool already exists from Plan 3. Verify it's wired:
  - Conversational feedback recognized in the loop ("that was helpful", "no, that's wrong") and logged via the tool.
  - Source set to `'conversational'`.
- WhatsApp reaction webhook integration: register the reaction events in the webhook handler from Plan 2 (this is a small extension; reactions arrive as a different webhook payload type). On reaction: locate the assistant message by ID, write a `feedback` row with `source='reaction'`, `target_type='message'`, sentiment derived from the emoji (👍/❤️ → positive, 👎 → negative, others → mixed).
- Admin view shows recent feedback grouped by sentiment.

### Operations polish
- **Monitoring**: document the external uptime + cron-ping setup (README ops section). Add a `/health/deep` endpoint that checks DB, Anthropic API reachability (lightweight ping, cached for 60s), and returns a JSON summary. Real alerting is external.
- **Backups**: Supabase has automated backups; document the tested-restore procedure. Add a small script that pulls a fresh dump and writes a checksum file — operators run it quarterly.
- **Staging path**: ability to test prompt changes against past message logs without sending output. Implement as a CLI: `python -m app.staging replay --prompt-version <name> --since <date> --user <id>` that runs the loop in a dry-run mode against historical messages, printing what it WOULD have sent and written. Does not call `send_outbound` or write to DB.
- **Daily LLM spend cap enforcement**: Plan 1 plumbed the helpers; now wire them into the actual call sites. Each Anthropic / OpenAI / Groq call checks `is_under_cap(provider)` first and degrades gracefully:
  - Conversational LLM cap hit → respond once with "I'm running into limits today, will catch up tomorrow" and queue silently.
  - Vision cap hit → fall back to "I see you sent an image — can you describe it?" (already wired in Plan 2; just verify cap check is in place).
  - Transcription cap hit → "I can't transcribe right now — can you send it as text?".
- **Alerts at 80% of cap**: when a per-provider per-day spend crosses 80%, log a distinguished WARNING line; surface the warning in the admin view's Spend page. External alerting is out of scope.

### Final polish
- README updates: deployment runbook, env var checklist (final), admin URL + password, how to read the admin views, how to run the staging CLI, backup procedure.
- "Open Decisions Before Build" from the spec: this plan does NOT implement the assistant's name (placeholder remains in the prompt), welcome copy is provided but flagged in README as "edit before launch", and weekly-summary timing per partner is configurable in env. The co-designer-vs-recipient question is explicitly out of scope (it's a non-technical decision for the user).

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- WhatsApp reactions sentiment refinement beyond the simple emoji map.
- Multi-tenant or per-couple isolation (the spec is for one couple).
- Anything not explicitly in the spec.

## Success criteria
- `must`: New user gets the welcome message on their first inbound and `users.onboarding_state` advances correctly.
- `must`: Admin view loads behind auth, all listed pages render, turn detail shows prompt, reasoning, and tool_calls.
- `must`: `/health/deep` returns a JSON summary including DB and Anthropic reachability.
- `must`: Reaction webhook produces a `feedback` row with the right sentiment derivation.
- `must`: All three provider caps are enforced in the call sites with the documented graceful degradation.
- `must`: Staging CLI runs the loop in dry-run mode against historical messages without writing or sending.
- `should`: Spend page shows per-provider daily usage with cap line and 80% warnings.
- `should`: README runbook is sufficient for the operator (the spec author) to deploy and operate without extra hand-holding.
- `info`: Backup checksum script exists.

## Notes
- Keep the admin UI dead simple. Bootstrap CSS or Pico.css from a CDN is fine. No JS framework.
- Auth: HTTP Basic Auth is acceptable given the small audience (one operator). Document [REDACTED].
- This is the final plan — leave the codebase in a launchable state with the README runbook complete.

Proceed with all tasks. Do not skip on permission grounds; you are running with MEGAPLAN_TRUSTED_CONTAINER=1 and have full workspace write access.

        "for key in ADMIN_PASSWORD [REDACTED] [REDACTED] ANTHROPIC_API_KEY OPENAI_API_KEY [REDACTED] VISION_DAILY_CAP_USD TRANSCRIPTION_DAILY_CAP_USD PARTNER_PHONE_A PARTNER_PHONE_B DATABASE_URL SUPABASE_URL [REDACTED] MEDIA_FETCH_TIMEOUT_S SCHEDULER_ENABLED SCHEDULER_POLL_INTERVAL_S SCHEDULER_BATCH_SIZE WEEKLY_SUMMARY_DEFAULT_DAY WEEKLY_SUMMARY_DEFAULT_TIME HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_HOURS SENTRY_DSN LOG_DESTINATION; do if grep -Eq \"^#?${key}=\" .env.example; then printf 'example %s\\n' \"$key\"; else printf 'example-missing %s\\n' \"$key\"; fi; done",

  "watch_items": [
    "Do not invoke nested megaplan tooling; treat this document as the execution briefing only.",
    "Onboarding must persist the inbound row before calling `send_outbound`, because free-form eligibility is derived from recent inbound rows.",
    "Onboarding welcome must be claimed atomically with `WHERE onboarding_state='pending' RETURNING id`; never send before the claim succeeds.",
    "A failed welcome send after the atomic claim should not reset onboarding or duplicate on webhook retry; admin/audit rows are the troubleshooting path.",
    "Keep media messages that are successfully enriched in a state the agentic loop can still consume; do not accidentally mark them processed in media handlers.",
    "Do not worsen existing pause/media/coalescer debt if touched while handling reactions or cap fallback.",
    "Admin UI must remain read-only, escaped, simple server-rendered HTML with Basic Auth on every subpage; no mutation forms or SPA build step.",
    "Audit output must include final outbound content and triggering inbound content, not only IDs, or it will not answer the operator's use case.",
    "Reaction feedback targets only known outbound assistant messages by WhatsApp message ID; unknown or non-whitelisted reactions should no-op without enqueueing the coalescer.",
    "Text cap fallback must not lose the original inbound burst and must not let startup recovery resend the cap notice repeatedly.",
    "Deferred retry needs a clear next-day cap reset schedule and recovery semantics; completed cap-hit turns and deferred messages must be excluded from crash/raw recovery.",
    "Spend warning logging should be once per provider/day after crossing 80 percent, not noisy on every subsequent cost record.",
    "Vision, transcription, and text provider cap checks must run before external provider calls, with tests proving the provider stubs are not invoked on cap hit.",
    "Staging replay safety must be by explicit dry-run sinks/adapters, not by a reject-on-write pool that aborts before producing candidate output or by a permissive pool that risks production writes.",
    "Dry-run write tools should return plausible results while recording WOULD-write entries; read tools may use the real DB.",
    "Anthropic deep health should be lightweight, short-timeout, and cached for 60 seconds to avoid noisy health checks or token spend where possible.",
    "README should flag welcome copy as edit-before-launch and keep assistant naming / co-designer-vs-recipient decisions out of technical scope.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: debouncer callback: revised step 8 defines `burstcoalescer.add(user_id, message_id)` but says expiry calls `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)`. i found no existing user model or repository helper in the current codebase, and the plan does not say whether the coalescer stores the user object, fetches it from the pool, or receives it in `add`, so the callback cannot reliably supply the `user` argument required by `run_agentic_turn` as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: edit idempotency: revised step 9 says the edit fixture reuses the original whatsapp message id, while revised step 10's normal message flow does `insert ... on conflict (whatsapp_message_id) do nothing` and treats no returned id as a redelivery. unless edit detection runs before the generic idempotent insert path, a real edit will be classified as a duplicate and skipped instead of updating `edit_history`, but the plan does not explicitly require that ordering. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `escalate_to_partner` sequence. step 5 constructs the `templatecall` and awaits `send_outbound(...)` before the text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate, so an implementation following the plan literally could send an unauthorized escalation before rejecting the tool call. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `tool_call_count` expectations against the spec. the plan's e2e criterion expects `tool_call_count == 3` because only write calls are counted, but the spec says `bot_turns` records tool count for admin visibility into pathological loops; excluding phase a read calls hides most loop activity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: query-level decay: `observations.significance` is nullable in `tool_schemas.py` and in current tests for failed scoring, and `scoring.rescore_observations` can leave it null after a failed re-score. the plan's sql function `recency_weighted_score(significance, last_reinforced_at, created_at)` does not specify null handling or `nulls last`; in postgres, ordering that nullable expression `desc` can surface null-scored failed observations before valid scored observations unless the executor adds `nulls last` or `coalesce`. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: escalation gate metadata: the plan says `escalate_to_partner` should accept `is_crisis=true` only when the current trigger charge is actually `crisis`, but the existing write-tool context only has `turncontext(triggering_message_ids, user, partner, phase)` and the charge is currently computed locally inside `agentic.run_agentic_turn` from hot context. the plan does not explicitly add that charge or an explicit-request marker to `turncontext` or the tool-call validator, so the proposed guard has no reliable data path in the current code shape. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] check-in-scheduling: check-in scheduling: one-pending-check-in invariant remains race-prone without a partial unique index or transactional locking strategy. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] debouncer: debouncer: coalescer cannot satisfy the `run_agentic_turn(message_ids, user)` callback contract from an `add(user_id, message_id)` api unless it stores or resolves the user object. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the search-before-write requirement against the new phase split. because phase b exposes only write/schedule tools, the model cannot recover by calling `get_memories`, `get_observations`, or `list_themes` if it realizes during phase b that it lacks enough read context to safely add versus update; this creates tension with the explicit agentic-search dedup requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: weekly summary timing: the spec requires the recurring weekly summary to fire at 09:00 local on the user's configured day, and the plan explicitly asks where that configuration should live. the implementation plan chooses global env defaults plus per-job context overrides, but `/pause` supersedes all pending jobs and `/resume` restores weekly summaries; without a durable per-user schedule field or an explicit rule to read the last superseded weekly job context, resume/startup seeding can silently lose the user's configured day. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: recovery/coalescer api mismatch: revised step 14 says recovery should re-add a whole `triggering_message_ids` list as one burst, but revised step 8 only specifies `add(user_id, message_id)` for one message at a time. calling `add` repeatedly can reset timers or merge with live inbound state, so the plan needs either an explicit `add_burst(user_id, ids)`/`fire(ids)` path or clear semantics for preserving a recovered burst as a single unit. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: indexing claim: revised step 2 says the new gin index supports recovery's `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)` lookup, but postgres gin indexes on arrays are normally used by array operators such as `@>`, `&&`, or `<@`, not by scalar `= any(array)` in a correlated subquery. the executor should either use an indexable predicate like `triggering_message_ids @> array[messages.id]` or drop the claim that this query is gin-backed. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked audit support around `prompt_snapshot`. the revised step 8 still opens `bot_turns` with `prompt_snapshot` before building hot context, while the spec's worked example calls for `<full prompt>` after hot context is loaded; unless the executor reorders this, the stored prompt snapshot will omit the per-turn hot context actually injected into the llm. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: check-in uniqueness: the success criteria require one pending check-in per user, and the current `schedule_checkin` implementation performs a separate `update scheduled_jobs set status='superseded'` followed by `insert into scheduled_jobs`. the plan later says to add db-level coverage, but the migration step only mentions indexes and does not specify a partial unique index such as one pending check-in per user, so two concurrent schedule calls can still race and leave multiple pending check-ins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
